Sec. 3. Notwithstanding the provisions of any other Executive Order, the functions of the President under the Federal Advisory Committee Act which are applicable to the committees listed in Sections 1 and 2 of this Order, except that of reporting annually to the Congress, shall be performed by the head of the department or agency designated after each committee, in accordance with guidelines and procedures established by the Administrator of General Services. Sec. 4. The following Executive Orders, that established committees which have terminated or whose work is completed, are revoked:

(a) Section 2(d) of Executive Order No. 12039, establishing the Intergovernmental Science, Engineering, and Technology Advisory Panel.

(b) Executive Order No. 12168, establishing the President's Commission for a National Agenda for the Eighties.

(c) Executive Order No. 12202, establishing the Nuclear Safety Oversight Committee.

(d) Executive Order No. 12229, establishing the White House Coal Advisory Council.

(e) Executive Order No. 12303, establishing the Presidential Advisory Committee on Federalism.

(f) Executive Order No. 12308, establishing the Presidential Task Force on the Arts and Humanities.

(g) Executive Order No. 12310, establishing the President's Commission on Housing.

(h) Executive Order No. 12323, establishing the Presidential Commission on Broadcasting to Cuba.


```

47207
Federal Register 
V ol. 46, N o. 186
Presidential Documents
Friday, September 25, 1981
Title 3— Executive Order 12323 of September 22, 1981
Hie President Presidential Commission on Broadcasting to Cuba
✓
By the authority vested in me as President by the Constitution of the United 
States of America, in accordance with the Federal Advisory Committee Act, 
as amended (5 U.S.C. App. I), and in order to create a commission to develop 
recommendations with respect to broadcasting of information and ideas to 
Cuba, it is hereby ordered as follows:
Section 1. E sta b lish m en t, (a) There is established the Presidential Commis­
sion on Broadcasting to Cuba.
(b) The Commission shall be composed of 11 members appointed by the 
President from among citizens of the United States.
(c) The President shall designate one of the members to be the Chairman.
(d) There shall be a three member Executive Committee of the Commission 
which shall be composed of the Chairman and two other members elected by 
the Commission.
Sec. 2. F unctions.
(a) The Commission shall develop a recommended plan for radio broadcasting 
intended for transmission to Cuba. The purpose of the plan shall be to 
promote open communication of information and ideas to Cuba and in particu­
lar broadcasting to the Cuban people of accurate information about Cuba.
(b) The Commission shall examine issues related to effectively carrying out 
such a plan for radio broadcasting to Cuba. These shall include, but not be 
limited to, possible program content, information gathering, writing and edit­
ing needs, staffing requirements, legal structure for a broadcasting organiza­
tion, proposed legislation, sample budgets, and the location, structure and 
function of possible broadcasting stations.
(c) The Commission may conduct studies, inquiries, hearings, and meetings as 
it deems necessary. It may assemble and disseminate information, and issue 
reports and other publications. It may also coordinate, sponsor, or oversee 
projects, studies, events and other activities that it deems necessary or 
desirable for development of a recommended program of broadcasting to 
Cuba.
(d) The Executive Committee may coordinate the work of the Commission and 
may act on behalf of the whole Commission.
(e) The Commission shall submit its recommendations and final report to the 
President and the Secretary of State no later than October 1,1982.
Sec. 3. A d m in istra tio n .
(a) Members of the Commission who are not otherwise Government employ­
ees shall serve without any compensation for their work on the Commission. 
However, all members shall be entitled to travel expenses, including per diem 
in lieu of subsistence, as authorized by law (5 U.S.C. 5701-5707).
(b) The heads of Executive agencies shall, to the extent permitted by law, 
provide the Commission such information and advice with respect to radio 
broadcasting to Cuba as the Commission may require for the purpose of 
carrying out its functions.
(c) Expenses of the Commission shall be paid from funds available to the 
Secretary of State.

=== PDF page 10 of 232 ===
47208 Federal Register /  Vol. 46, No. 186 / Friday, September 25, 1981 /  Presidential Documents
Sec. 4. G eneral.
(a) Notwithstanding any other Executive order, the functions of the President 
under the Federal Advisory Committee Act, as amended (5 U.S.C. App. I), 
which are applicable to the Commission, except that of reporting annually to 
the Congress, shall be performed by the Secretary of State in accordance with 
guidelines and procedures established by the Administrator of General Serv­
ices.
(b) The Commission shall terminate on October 1, 1982, or 60 days after 
submitting its final report, whichever is earlier.
THE WHITE HOUSE, 
S ep tem b er 22, 1981.
[FR Doc. 81-28095 
Filed 9-24-81; 10:00 am] 
Billing code 3195-01-M

Federal Register / Vol. 48, No. 3 / Wednesday, January 5,1983 / Presidential Documents 379
Presidential Documents
Executive Order 12399 of December 31, 1982
Continuance of Certain Federal Advisory Committees
By the authority vested in me as President by the Constitution and statutes of 
the United States of America, and in accordance,with the provisions of the  
Federal Advisory Committee Act, as amended (5 U.S.C. App. I), it is hereby  
ordered as follows:
Section 1. Each advisory committee listed below is continued until September  
30,1984.
(a) Committee for the Preservation of the White House; Executive Order No.  
11145, as amended (Department of the Interior).
(b) President’s Commission on White House Fellowships; Executive Order No.  
11183, as amended (Office of Personnel Management).
(c) President’s Committee on the National Medal of Science; Executive Order  
No. 11287, as amended (National Science Foundation).
(d) President’s Council on Physical Fitness and Sports; Executive Order No.  
12345, as amended (Department of Health and Human Services).
(e) President’s Committee on Mental Retardation; Executive Order No. 11776  
(Department of Health and Human Services).
(f) President’s Export Council; Executive Order No. 12131 (Department of  
Commerce).
(g) The International Private Enterprise Task Force; Executive Order No. 12395  
(Agency for International Development).
(h) Advisory Committee on Small and Minority Business Ownership; Execu­
tive Order No. 12190 (Small Business Administration).
(i) Federal Advisory Council on Occupational Safety and Health; Executive  
Order No. 12196 (Department of Labor).
(j) President’s Committee on the International Labor Organization; Executive  
Order No. 12216 (Department of Labor).
(k) President’s Economic Policy Advisory Board; Executive Order No. 12296  
(Office of Policy Development).
(l) National Productivity Advisory Committee; Executive Order No. 12332  
(Department of the Treasury).
(m) President’s Committee on the Arts and the Humanities; Executive Order  
No. 12367 (National Endowment for the Arts).
Sec. 2. The following advisory committee is continued until December 31,1983:  
President’s National Security Telecommunications Advisory Committee; Ex­
ecutive Order No. 12382 (Department of Defense).
Sec. 3. Notwithstanding the provisions of any other Executive Order, the  
functions of the President under the Federal Advisory Committee Act which 
are applicable to the committees listed in Sections 1 and 2 of this Order,  
except that of reporting annually to the Congress, shall be performed by the  
head of the department or agency designated after each committee, in accord­
ance with guidelines and procedures established by the Administrator of  
General Services.

=== PDF page 10 of 248 ===
380 Federal Register / Vol. 48, No. 3 / Wednesday, January 5, 1983 / Presidential Documents
Sec. 4. The following Executive Orders, that established committees which  
have terminated or whose work is completed, are revoked:
(a) Section 2(d) of Executive Order No. 12039, establishing the Intergovern­
mental Science, Engineering, and Technology Advisory Panel.
(b) Executive Order No. 12168, establishing the President’s Commission for a  
National Agenda for the Eighties.
(c) Executive Order No. 12202, establishing the Nuclear Safety Oversight  
Committee.
(d) Executive Order No. 12229, establishing the White House Coal Advisory  
Council.
(e) Executive Order No. 12303, establishing the Presidential Advisory Commit­
tee on Federalism.
(f) Executive Order No. 12308, establishing the Presidential Task Force on the  
Arts and Humanities.
(g) Executive Order No. 12310, establishing the President’s Commission on  
Housing.
(h) Executive Order No. 12323, establishing the Presidential Commission on  
Broadcasting to Cuba.
(i) Executive Order No. 12329, establishing the President’s Task Force on  
Private Sector Initiatives.
(j) Executive Order No. 12360, establishing the President’s Task Force on  
Victims of Crime.
(k) Section 1-2 of Executive Order No. 12137, as amended, establishing the  
Peace Corps Advisory Council.
Sec. 5. Executive Order No. 12258 is’ superseded.
Sec. 6. This Order shall be effective December 31,1982.
THE WHITE HOUSE,  
D ecem b er 31, 1982.
[FR Doc. 83-401 
Filed 1-4-83; 12:03 pm ] 
Billing code 3195-01-M

```

23135
Federal Register 
Vol. 47, No. 163 
Thursday, May 27, 1982
Presidential Documents
Title 3—
The President
Executive Order 1236è of M ay 25, 1982
Presidential Commission on Broadcasting to Cuba
By the authority vested in me as President of the United States of A m erica,  
and in accordance with the Federal A dvisory Committee A ct, as amended (5  
U.S.C. App. 1), in order to permit an increase in the membership of the  
Presidential Commission on Broadcasting to Cuba by one member, Section  
1(b) of Executive Order No. 12323 of September 22,1981, is hereby amended to  
read as follows:
“(b) The Commission shall be composed of twelve members appointed by the  
President from among citizens of the United States.”
THE W H ITE HOUSE,  
M a y  25, 1982.
[FR Doc. 82-14684 
Filed 5-28-82; 9:03 am] 
Billing code 3195-01-M

chronology does not decide legal effect.","provision_text":"Sec. 3. Notwithstanding the provisions of any other Executive Order, the functions of the President under the Federal Advisory Committee Act which are applicable to the committees listed in Sections 1 and 2 of this Order, except that of reporting annually to the Congress, shall be performed by the head of the department or agency designated after each committee, in accordance with guidelines and procedures established by the Administrator of General Services. Sec. 4. The following Executive Orders, that established committees which have terminated or whose work is completed, are revoked:\n\n(a) Section 2(d) of Executive Order No. 12039, establishing the Intergovernmental Science, Engineering, and Technology Advisory Panel.\n\n(b) Executive Order No. 12168, establishing the President's Commission for a National Agenda for the Eighties.\n\n(c) Executive Order No. 12202, establishing the Nuclear Safety Oversight Committee.\n\n(d) Executive Order No. 12229, establishing the White House Coal Advisory Council.\n\n(e) Executive Order No. 12303, establishing the Presidential Advisory Committee on Federalism.\n\n(f) Executive Order No. 12308, establishing the Presidential Task Force on the Arts and Humanities.\n\n(g) Executive Order No. 12310, establishing the President's Commission on Housing.\n\n(h) Executive Order No. 12323, establishing the Presidential Commission on Broadcasting to Cuba.\n\n","sections_touched":["Sections 1","Section 2(d)"],"signing_date":"1982-12-31","source":{"acceptable_as_original":false,"archival_id":"app:node/245060","filing_date":null,"grade":"mirror_transcription","instrument_id":"EO:12399","location_confidence":"medium","location_verified":false,"page_or_item":"Executive Order 12399—Continuance of Certain Federal Advisory Committees; Notwithstanding the provisions of any other Executive Order, the functions of the President under the Federal Advisory Committee Act which are applicable to the committees listed in Sections 1 and 2 of this Order, except that of reporting annually to the Congress, shall be performed by the head of the department or agency designated after each committee, in accordance with guidelines and procedures established by the Administrator of General Services. Sec. 4. The order establishes the Presidential Commission on Broadcasting to Cuba of eleven members (Section 1), its functions of developing a plan for radio broadcasting to Cuba (Section 2, with the final report due by October 1, 1982), administration (Section 3) and general provisions (Section 4). EO 12366 later raised the membership to twelve. First end: Section 4(b) of the order provides that the Commission shall terminate on October 1, 1982, or 60 days after submitting its final report, whichever is earlier. The fixed date is the latest date on which the Commission could end, so I record 1982-10-01 as the first end by the order's own terms (read inclusively: the Commission exists through October 1, 1982; the exclusive reading would put it at the start of that day). The report-relative alternative could have ended it earlier; the held packet has no dated record of the final report, so that possibility is disclosed and no earlier date is claimed. Section 4(h) of EO 12399 later revoked the order, effective December 31, 1982, which is after the sunset and is a later event.  Machine searches adopted after reading their coverage notes: the U.S. Code search (release point 119-121, 0 hits for this order), the eCFR search as of 2026-10-14 (0 hits) and the Federal Register executive-order catalog disposition-note search (5 hits). They are leads only; the end, the later events and the savings were decided from the held Federal Register texts named in this claim. Search of the held order texts for this order's number (the packet records a bounded number-citation search over its lookup corpus, excluding clause-draft copies): apart from the order's own copies, the matching files are EO 12366, EO 12399.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Search of the held order texts for this order's number (the packet records a bounded number-citation search over its lookup corpus, excluding clause-draft copies): apart from the order's own copies, the matching files are EO 12366, EO 12399. Section 4(b) is the order's own end; Section 2(e) sets the report deadline at October 1, 1982 as well, which is a deadline and not an end. EO 12366 amends Section 1(b) only.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The report duty in Section 2(e) and the administrative commands in Section 3 are inseparable from the Commission and end with it; no held record shows any of them spent before October 1, 1982.\",\"savings_check\":\"I read the full held text of EO 12399 (Federal Register text, 48 FR 379-380); it contains no saving, pending-matters or transition clause for the orders it revokes, so nothing outlives the revocation by its own terms. The order also has no saving clause of its own.\",\"scope_check\":\"The own-terms end in Section 4(b) covers the whole order. The dossier inventory has seventeen clause rows covering the held original text, which I confirmed; two of them, the Sec. 2 and Sec. 4 headings, are too short to quote alone and an inventory override folds each into the first item under it, leaving fifteen clauses. ntial-commission-broadcasting-cuba.txt","sha256":"95cc04e61ded6a5db352f410d9a69f54372a4bf8a91403531b1fa1a2364a96bd","state":"match"},"sha256":"b1743ec8a5458ede9fd1a537dd491733d1a9504ec77282964949e51ab27eb2cd","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-12366-presidential-commission-broadcasting-cuba.txt","text_sha256":"95cc04e61ded6a5db352f410d9a69f54372a4bf8a91403531b1fa1a2364a96bd"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"65e540426eaa44b46086d4a71266b9dfd2f381f28f04cf4831455f8e8f3016d3","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12399.txt","sha256":"65e540426eaa44b46086d4a71266b9dfd2f381f28f04cf4831455f8e8f3016d3","state":"match"},"sha256":"4330a1aa743f228ed915aa5a8b604a42291e75b8dfd394d74d60d66c22e39408","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12399.txt","text_sha256":"65e540426eaa44b46086d4a71266b9dfd2f381f28f04cf4831455f8e8f3016d3"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"95cc04e61ded6a5db352f410d9a69f54372a4bf8a91403531b1fa1a2364a96bd","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-12366-presidential-commission-broadcasting-cuba.txt","sha256":"95cc04e61ded6a5db352f410d9a69f54372a4bf8a91403531b1fa1a2364a96bd","state":"match"},"sha256":"b1743ec8a5458ede9fd1a537dd491733d1a9504ec77282964949e51ab27eb2cd","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-12366-presidential-commission-broadcasting-cuba.txt","text_sha256":"95cc04e61ded6a5db352f410d9a69f54372a4bf8a91403531b1fa1a2364a96bd"}]},{"claim_sha256":"511e3049c309ff5b8bcb10b2cfedeeac6ce0d92c257afd65c17a88cbe919f9a7","current_canonical":true,"locations":[{"file_sha256":"f9807850c6ac9078fbbef0dac3e1b659c0793aa076b230e4664021259d2da85c","path":"research/readiness/claims/claude-2026-11-05-theophilus-r03.jsonl","pointer":"line:1"}],"reported_material_gaps":[],"research_rationale":"EO 12323 (Presidential Commission on Broadcasting to Cuba), signed 1981-09-22. The order establishes the Presidential Commission on Broadcasting to Cuba of eleven members (Section 1), its functions of developing a plan for radio broadcasting to Cuba (Section 2, with the final report due by October 1, 1982), administration (Section 3) and general provisions (Section 4). EO 12366 later raised the membership to twelve. First end: Section 4(b) of the order provides that the Commission shall terminate on October 1, 1982, or 60 days after submitting its final report, whichever is earlier. The fixed date is the latest date on which the Commission could end, so I record 1982-10-01 as the first end by the order's own terms (read inclusively: the Commission exists through October 1, 1982; the exclusive reading would put it at the start of that day). The report-relative alternative could have ended it earlier; the held packet has no dated record of the final report, so that possibility is disclosed and no earlier date is claimed. Section 4(h) of EO 12399 later revoked the order, effective December 31, 1982, which is after the sunset and is a later event.  Machine searches adopted after reading their coverage notes: the U.S. Code search (release point 119-121, 0 hits for this order), the eCFR search as of 2026-10-14 (0 hits) and the Federal Register executive-order catalog disposition-note search (5 hits). They are leads only; the end, the later events and the savings were decided from the held Federal Register texts named in this claim. Search of the held order texts for this order's number (the packet records a bounded number-citation search over its lookup corpus, excluding clause-draft copies): apart from the order's own copies, the matching files are EO 12366, EO 12399.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Search of the held order texts for this order's number (the packet records a bounded number-citation search over its lookup corpus, excluding clause-draft copies): apart from the order's own copies, the matching files are EO 12366, EO 12399. Section 4(b) is the order's own end; Section 2(e) sets the report deadline at October 1, 1982 as well, which is a deadline and not an end. EO 12366 amends Section 1(b) only.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The report duty in Section 2(e) and the administrative commands in Section 3 are inseparable from the Commission and end with it; no held record shows any of them spent before October 1, 1982.\",\"savings_check\":\"I read the full held text of EO 12399 (Federal Register text, 48 FR 379-380); it contains no saving, pending-matters or transition clause for the orders it revokes, so nothing outlives the revocation by its own terms. The order also has no saving clause of its own.\",\"scope_check\":\"The own-terms end in Section 4(b) covers the whole order. The dossier inventory has seventeen clause rows covering the held original text, which I confirmed; two of them, the Sec. 2 and Sec. 4 headings, are too short to quote alone and an inventory override folds each into the first item under it, leaving fifteen clauses. EO 12366 (Section 1(b)) and EO 12399 (Section 4(h)) are recorded as later events.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"eb37832760863d50413742177a4bdb54fc1650aa7648319f927faa633958b7d4","path":"research/excavator/claude-theophilus-2026-11-05/r03/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW_A.jsonl","pointer":"line:10","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"b2df108241450795c1479c60602cce389eba8465f30715b9c6e713dc8434e1dc","path":"research/excavator/claude-theophilus-2026-11-05/r03/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW_B.jsonl","pointer":"line:10","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:12323#sec1"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:12323#sec1"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:12323#sec1-b"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:12323#sec1-b"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:12323#sec1-c"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:12323#sec1-c"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:12323#sec1-d"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:12323#sec1-d"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:12323#sec2-a"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:12323#sec2-a"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:12323#sec2-b"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:12323#sec2-b"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:12323#sec2-c"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:12323#sec2-c"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:12323#sec2-d"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:12323#sec2-d"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:12323#sec2-e"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:12323#sec2-e"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:
